mr president, i would like to thank you. as several speakers have already pointed out, the digital revolution is a fact which is affecting not only the way we consume but also the way we work and learn. digitalization is changing the way we live. however, the commission's communication focuses only on the traveller consumer and thus lacks any analysis of, for example, the impact on the labour market and working conditions. digitalisation and the sharing economy bring opportunities as well as challenges. the boundaries between workers and contractors are blurred. this makes the application of labour law and collective agreements more difficult. it can be very unclear what is really the case. remote and crowdworking create new challenges and risks, both in the mental and physical working environment. more flexible forms of work can be very positive, but we can never accept that the trend towards more flexibility through digitalisation also leads to more precarious employment or less protection for workers.
mr president, to complete the single market is the highway to jobs and growth. let us have the courage to be disruptive and to stand up for an open, free and global internet. we liberate the forces that exist and boost trust by removing the digital barriers and borders. consumers should not pay twice for the same service, depending on where they live in europe, and our smes should not abide by 28 sets of rules. to create scale, they need one digital market. i support this report as it addresses national exceptions and protectionism. europe will become competitive through simple, modern and clear rules, not through shutting out others. our problem is fragmentation, not innovation. we should not slow down the shared economy but ensure that transparency and a free flow of data is as important as the other four freedoms. the same rule should apply online and offline. let me conclude by saying that swiftly reaching an agreement on safe harbour is crucial in order to avoid a legal vacuum. time is running out.
- i've put my voice down. the report is based on the eu's market-liberal doctrines. the commission has already rejected at committee level several of the gue/ngl amendments which sought to tighten up the rules, for example against social dumping and strong measures to combat tax evasion by transnational companies. i am in favour of mandatory public country-specific reporting of profits and taxes and of increased coordination and exchange of information between member states to counter tax evasion and aggressive tax planning by large companies. but i am opposed to proposals which aim to give the eu power over tax policy and, in the long term, taxation rights. i therefore choose to abstain.
in writing. we regret that the annual competition report focuses so much on successful entrepreneurship in the digital economy and seeks to regulate rather than create conditions for new entrepreneurship and increased competition. we also want to draw attention to a major competition problem in the energy union which will have to be addressed in next year's competition report, namely gazprom's attempts to monopolize and increase its dependence on russian gas. it is contrary both to internal energy market legislation and to european security interests. gazprom's deliberate attempt at market dominance has a scope and significance far greater for the european market than that of successful digital companies constantly exposed to new competition but developing through their capacity for innovation.
‒ i voted in favour of the report. the report addresses important issues of cultural and social marginalisation and how exposure can lead to radicalisation. the report also notes the rise in racism and xenophobia and calls for financial resources to combat them. despite these positive aspects, the report maintains an uncritical approach to intercultural dialogue. in particular, the report considers that europe is the owner of the values that other groups need to adopt, using terms such as 'exposing children to other cultures' and 'segregated populations, behaving in a way that is contrary to european fundamental values'. this consistent attitude shows that the european parliament still has a lot of work to do in terms of critically examining its own cultural heritage, and not just assuming that the problems are with 'the others'. despite these negative aspects, i have chosen to vote in favour of the report because it seeks to promote dialogue rather than polarization and discrimination and to allocate more resources to culture and education.
mr president, i fully support this report because women’s entrepreneurship is an enormous untapped potential for growth and jobs. it is a matter of equality and empowerment. we are not a vulnerable group. women are important actors in our economy, an asset for our economies, and the time has come for member states to recognise the full potential of female entrepreneurship and to adopt clear strategies to remove all forms of discrimination and existing obstacles, from facilitating access to capital and reducing red tape to any other form of discrimination. we must also combat stereotypes. women are too often seen as less credible, not least in sectors such as innovation and technology. it is crucial to increase business training and education, and to develop a culture of second chance, so that women can believe that they can do it and start a company. i welcome more networks of women, because it is important to support each other, to exchange good practices and to learn from each other. member states must also create the conditions for women to better conciliate family and work, but this is an issue that has to be dealt with at national level.
‒ today, parliament voted on a report on obstacles to female entrepreneurship in europe. the report highlights pressing issues, not least reforms to boost entrepreneurship in women-dominated sectors. unfortunately, there were also some paragraphs that i am very critical of. this includes the need for eu regulation of parental leave. the member states, like a broad public opinion throughout the union, have rejected the idea of the so-called maternity leave directive. the fact that parliament is now pressing for a new process to be started on eu-regulated parental insurance is both unfortunate and fundamentally provocative. i voted against the paragraphs that raised this. but as they represented a very small part of the report, i still voted in favour of the final vote.
in writing. - for me, equality is a matter of the heart. the fall in the proportion of women entrepreneurs in all member states is a loss for society as a whole. i fully share the view that there is a need for a holistic approach to women's entrepreneurship and that women's access to eu funding and further development opportunities should be facilitated in various ways. i do not think that gender quotas are part of the solution, however. i therefore voted against paragraph 22 of this report, but i nevertheless chose to endorse the report as a whole because it contains other important elements of importance for women entrepreneurs.

‒ the european parliament voted today on an own-initiative report on skills-building measures to combat youth unemployment. the report mentioned several issues in education policy that are not within the eu's competence. i voted against them, such as the european youth guarantee and the investment in apprenticeships and training in countries such as greece with high youth unemployment. i also voted against a new eu prize for the best project to combat youth unemployment. in the final vote, however, i voted for the report as a whole, as it did not contain any sharp proposals that would imply a new eu policy in the field of education or the labour market.
in writing. - i've turned my voice down. with youth unemployment in the eu at 21.4%, strong action and new economic thinking emphasising public green investment for new jobs and a developed welfare sector are needed. this report does not advocate such a new policy, but instead focuses on individual skills development and the promotion of entrepreneurship. but at the same time i note the improvements made in the committee's work on the report, which now places greater emphasis on the rights of young people in the labour market. that's why i choose to keep my voice down.
in writing. ‒ reducing youth unemployment in europe is absolutely necessary. the exclusion of millions of young people from the labour market is harmful in several ways. it is harmful to the economies of member states with large numbers of people of working age outside the labour market, to companies that are unable to recruit new and skilled workers, and above all to young people themselves who are given a poor start in life. this report therefore highlights a number of good points, including the need to link the labour market and education more closely together. i voted for the report as a matter of course, but i voted against the introduction of a prize for projects that are good at fighting unemployment. i do not think that is the role of the eu and i am convinced that the member states value getting young people into work more than getting a prize.
in writing. - i've lowered my voice. the report is not clear enough on the issue of net neutrality. the amendments tabled by the gue/ngl group to ensure this were unfortunately rejected at committee level. i want new technology to benefit all eu citizens. it should not be the interests of the telecom companies that are prioritized. i would also like to stress the importance of personal privacy, data security and safety and health issues in this context. as for the question of geo-blocking, it has many dimensions. the swedish journalists' association has written to the swedish parliament, stressing the problem of a responsible publisher in sweden not being able to use geo-blocking. they write a swedish website very well be subject to legal review in other european countries, at least if the material is available for reading, watching, listening in that country. in this context, we consider it to be almost self-evident that the owner/publisher of this website must be able to decide, for example by means of geo-blocking, in which territories certain media content may or may not be made available. this is a matter which must be thoroughly investigated before it is time for legislation. i choose to keep my voice down.

‒ today the european parliament voted on a record in the review of the eu biodiversity strategy. the report contains several important elements, such as the continuing ban on certain toxic spraying agents. the report also rejects a revision of the habitats and birds directives and calls for common sustainability criteria for forestry. the habitats directive needs to be revised, not least because it over-regulates wildlife management (including wolf hunting). a stance we pushed hard for during the campaign. the birds directive is from 1979 and the habitats directive is from 1992, and which species need protection must be updated regularly. some species that need protection get nothing, and some species that get protection no longer need it. the report also calls for common criteria for sustainable forestry. but the conditions for forestry in sweden and, for example, greece are very different. it is not in the interests of the biodiversity of greek or swedish forests to transfer more power over forestry policy to the eu. i therefore voted against the report.
in writing. - biodiversity is important. but today we voted against the european parliament's involvement in the review of the eu biodiversity strategy. the reason is that it rejects a revision of the habitats and birds directives and calls for common sustainability criteria for forestry. nature is changing and we must therefore be prepared to revise our legislation to better reflect the reality of today. only then can we ensure biodiversity and sustainable management of the wild stock. the birds directive is from 1979 and the habitats directive is from 1992, and which species need protection must be updated regularly. some species that need protection get nothing, and some species that get protection no longer need it. the european parliament also called for common criteria for sustainable forestry. but the conditions for forestry in sweden and, for example, greece are very different. it is not in the interests of the biodiversity of greek or swedish forests to transfer more power over forestry policy to the eu.
‒ generally a good report on ecosystem services and their role in nature, the economy and society. for example, antibiotic resistance is mentioned as a future challenge. the species and habitats directive regulates wolf stocks to some extent, and it is deeply unfortunate that this report opposes a review of the legislation. however, there are reports that the commission needs to review what should apply when adapting the species and habitats directive to, for example, economic and regional conditions. we see the need to control our wolf tribe as part of our special condition. i would have preferred other texts in paragraphs 65 and 66 which would be more open to revision so that regional considerations can be safeguarded. the report contains a number of articles on sustainability criteria for biomass and forest management. its importance will depend entirely on its design and it is important that these do not disadvantage sustainable production from scandinavia and respect that forestry policy is a competence of the member states. i voted against the reports on the opening up of the rules on environmental inspections on the problem of pesticide resistance, because i do not think it is justified to change the framework for that reason. overall, the report contains important comments on the considerations that should be taken into account in the review of the eu biodiversity strategy. in view of the above, i have therefore voted in favour of the proposal.

in writing. 'the own-initiative report is not legislation and is mainly concerned with taking action against the serious depletion of europe's biodiversity. of course we stand up for the swedish forest and the right of sweden to decide for itself about the hunt of wolves. the eu has no competence in hunting matters it is a national matter. when eu lawyers use the nature conservation directives as an argument to stop swedish wolf hunting, they are wrong. a review of the directives in that area is therefore welcome. but the report is also good and the eu must do more to address the depletion of biodiversity in many eu countries. sweden is far ahead, not least because of our forestry policy, which combines biodiversity and the use of forest resources. the protection of biodiversity is vital for europe's future, but current efforts are insufficient. the depletion of ecosystems also has social consequences, especially in rural areas. countless jobs depend on biodiversity and nature. we take responsibility for the eu's commitment to sustainable, environmentally friendly and social development. we therefore support the report, even if we do not share all its positions.'
in writing. ‒ a modern nature management system is based on the principle of adaptive management. the natural values we see today are the product of a management of the natural resources we had. it is of the utmost importance that management is adapted to actual changes and to reality. that flexibility is lacking today. we have a rigid legislation which in practice means that we have to invest time and resources in species which do not need extended protection, while there are species which are currently being hunted but which from a scientific point of view should not be hunted. this is serious. it is necessary to introduce the possibility of amending the annexes to the directive on endangered species fairly quickly and without having to amend the whole directive. writing about the need for the european commission to review what should apply when adapting the species and habitats directive to, for example, economic and regional conditions is in my view insufficient. however, this is a good report overall, and although i would have preferred other texts in paragraphs 65 and 66 which would be more open to revision so that regional considerations can be safeguarded, i choose to vote in favour of the proposal.

mr president, i should say in this debate that the european union is not working as it should. decisions are not being taken, although this is necessary and urgent. but the biggest problem is not in the european institutions but in the member states and their governments. in country after country the discussion revolves around ‘me’ and ‘we’ against ‘them’, against the refugees and against the other countries. we are asking the wrong questions. in sweden, in great britain and in poland we should instead be asking ourselves what our own responsibilities are, what leadership we can take and how we can contribute to making our part of the world a little better. great britain is a good example. it has a proud, honourable people and it should not be the hit-and-run driver. it should be the leader in europe and, instead, take that role for the future.
in writing. ‒ back in 2014, sweden democrats opposed the association agreement between the eu and georgia due to concerns over georgia’s future accession to the eu. when this agreement was signed both parties (the eu and georgia) agreed on a preferential trade regime. however the agreement contains a clause (article 27) called the ‘anti-circumvention mechanism’ which establishes an average annual volume of imports from georgia into the eu of certain agricultural products. when the volume of imports exceeds the annual volume georgia must provide the eu with justification that georgia has the capacity to produce the products concerned in excess, otherwise the eu may temporarily suspend preferential treatment for the products concerned for a period of six months. the anti-circumvention mechanism would therefore limit the amount of imports of certain agricultural products from georgia to the eu and would also avoid imports of non-georgian products via georgia using the advantages of the agreement’s provisions. to prevent a possible negative impact on the swedish market and more importantly to protect our swedish farmers’ best interests, sweden democrats supports this proposal for a regulation providing for the temporary suspension of tariff preferences of the association agreement between the eu and georgia.
in writing. – back in 2014, the sweden democrats opposed the association agreement between the eu and georgia due to concerns of georgia’s future accession to the eu. when this agreement was signed both parties (the eu and georgia) agreed on preferential trade regime. however the agreement contains a clause (article 27) that is called an ‘anti-circumvention mechanism’ which establishes an average annual volume of imports from georgia into the eu of certain agricultural products. when the volume of imports exceeds the annual volume, georgia shall provide the eu with a justification that georgia has the capacity to produce the products concerned in excess. by an absence of a duly justification the eu may temporarily suspend the preferential treatment for the products concerned for a period of 6 months. the anti-circumvention mechanism would therefore limit the amount of imports of certain agricultural products from georgia to the eu and would also avoid imports of non-georgian products via georgia using the advantages of the agreement’s provisions. in order to prevent a possible negative impact on the swedish market and more importantly to protect our swedish farmers’ best interests the sweden democrats are supporting this proposal for regulation providing the temporary suspension of tariff preferences of the association agreement between the eu and georgia.
‒ i voted against the objection to authorise the genetically modified soybean, mainly because we have to rely on science, i.e. if the european food safety authority (efsa) after evaluation considers a crop to be safe, that is what we should take as a starting point and nothing else. with a growing population and increasing climate threats, modern plant breeding is needed in europe. this includes modern techniques for growing crops and other plants that can feed the world's population and contribute to a better climate.

‒ the decision taken by the commission and the member states in the technical committee for motor vehicles on flexibility for car emission limits undermines the current legislation, which was already adopted in 2007. this is without any scientific basis being presented for the decision, and despite the fact that the technology to comply with the current limit values already exists, is not very expensive and is already being applied by several car manufacturers. the decision cannot therefore be interpreted as anything other than a concession to the part of the automotive industry that chose to invest in cheating the defective tests instead of investing in making its cars more environmentally friendly to meet the legal requirements and consumer expectations. the decision is thus a slap in the face to the responsible and environmentally conscious part of the automotive industry by depriving it of the rewards of its investment by virtually doubling the emission limits by 2020. for these reasons, i have voted to veto the decision.

for the alde group. president. - the debate is closed. i agree with what has been said: that the illegal trade in pet animals is a serious public health risk. it is not just the spread of diseases such as rabies, but also antibiotic resistance. i also agree with those who say that the illegal trade is a significant source of income for the mafia in europe. that's also true, that's right. it is obvious that many member states today have shortcomings in the control of their pet animals. tighter rules on data-driven registration would reduce this problem, but all this must be done at member state level. a european database would have no added value at all, it would cost more than it creates and it is very unclear who will create it? who's going to update it? and how is that to be done at all? forgive me for saying this, but we are not even able to register all the refugees who come to europe. and now some people want us to try and register all the cats and dogs that are in europe. it's impossible to do, simply impossible. this is the kind of task the eu should not be doing. the new eu animal health act, for which i have taken responsibility, is about to be finally adopted by the european parliament. it allows the commission to require countries to establish national databases on dogs and cats. so what you're asking for is already there. the new law also implies more measures and registration requirements to tackle the illegal trade we have heard about and which was discussed here: the problem of stray dogs. so the tools to solve the problem will soon be in place, if we can just hold out for a while until they become effective. mr president, the terrorist attacks in tunisia have had a tremendous impact on that country’s already weak economy. therefore, the commission’s initiative on additional exports of olive oil is to be welcomed. i hear some honourable members complaining about the disastrous impact that additional imports will have, but the numbers tell a different story. europeans are consuming more or less the same amount of oil as every year, but our production has decreased by almost 50% and the amount of oil that will eventually come into the eu from tunisia is about 1.7%. my question to these members is: in what way will one additional per cent destroy a european market which is lacking 48%? i can tell you how it will affect tunisians: olive oil employs more than one million people; it is tunisia’s main agricultural export, it brings growth, and it prevents despair. for us it is nothing, but for tunisia it is a big deal. i hope that parliament will show the promised solidarity in the vote tomorrow.

madam president, i would like to thank you. the concept of international refugee protection has gradually changed and now includes a very distorted interpretation of the convention on human rights. the problem is that the majority of the refugees we take in are actually economic migrants, and only 10 to 20 percent of those who come here are granted refugee status. a large number of people who are not entitled to asylum in europe must therefore expect to be returned - often under duress. from a human rights perspective, return cannot be seen as an offence and the ombudsman's proposal that frontex should set up a special service to receive and deal with complaints from migrants who consider themselves ill-treated during return is the result of a policy that is not realistic. personally, i feel that the proposal is just another piece of the machinery of those politicians who are against the organised and effective return of illegal migrants.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the introduction of urgent autonomous trade measures for tunisia. tunisia's economy has been hit hard by the terrorist attacks. the commission proposes zero tariffs for imports of olives from tunisia for the period 1 january 2016 to 31 december 2017 for the levels already agreed for imports of olives. olive growing accounts for one fifth of tunisia's agricultural employment and employs more than one million people. the left party supports increased trade in the agricultural sector for this region. however, the very central and important issue of debt cancellations was not addressed. i believe that debt relief for tunisia is a better way of supporting the country's economy, both in the short and long term, and i would like to see an economic policy that goes in that direction.
‒ repeated terrorist attacks have dealt a severe blow to tunisia's already weak economy. the eu commission has therefore proposed temporary measures to support tunisia. i am convinced that an annual duty-free import quota of 35 000 tonnes of olive oil will be of great importance to tunisia, since olive oil is the country's main export product and the sector employs about one million people. i do not share the view that the proposal has a detrimental effect on the eu market, since eu own production has recently, for various reasons, decreased by some 48%, and tunisian olive oil accounts for some 1,7% of european consumption. these temporary measures are not sufficient, but i still choose to support the proposal, regretting that protectionism has managed to creep into the final resolution.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of this report today. i am doing so in order to provide the workers in this case with financial support for retraining and/or other measures to find new jobs. however, i would like to stress that the widespread unemployment in the eu could be effectively combated if the austerity policies pursued for a long time by the commission and the governments of the member states were stopped. to create new jobs in europe, a new economic policy with massive public and environmentally friendly investments is needed. investments for the benefit of society are required not only in education, health and care, but also in infrastructure, railways, public transport, energy-efficient housing and sustainable energy systems.
‒ free trade is a continuation of economic prosperity and in turn individual freedom. that is why i strongly object to the european globalisation adjustment fund, which is an expression of protectionism. for this reason, i believe that the fund should be wound up in an orderly manner.
in writing. - i voted against all three reports. the basic tone of all three reports is one of support for the economic policy which sets the framework for the european semester. i am opposed to the eu's market-liberal line, austerity policy, privatisation and deregulation. opposition to this policy is growing throughout the eu. i note that in the reports, particularly those by mr rodrigues, there are attempts to criticise the policy pursued and some arguments in favour of stimulus measures. however, it is not clear enough for me to vote in favour.
‒ we voted in favour of the european parliament's report on the commission's annual growth survey. we are opposed to proposals that the eu budget could be used to offset national budgets through own resources. it ignores the different responsibilities and the competences at national and community level, as well as the extent of the common budget in relation to the national ones. of course, increased spending on fisheries, regional policy or research does not offset the costs of schooling, education, police, justice, defence or social security. rather, it is important that the common budget focuses on the common tasks at eu level and on the major future investments needed rather than subsidising agriculture and old business structures, while safeguarding important commitments and the conditions for economic growth through responsible budgetary policies and structural reforms in the member states. that is how we can face the economic problems that many member states are facing.
‒ we are in favour of raising minimum wages in the eu to reduce poverty and combat wage dumping. unlike in most countries, there is no statutory minimum wage in sweden, but wages are set by collective agreements between the social partners without direct political interference. we therefore believe that minimum wages should not be regulated by law, but that national practices in each individual member state must be taken into account in order to strengthen collective agreements and workers' rights in the different wage systems.
in writing. - i voted against all three reports. the basic tone of all three reports is one of support for the economic policy which sets the framework for the european semester. i am opposed to the eu's market-liberal line, austerity policy, privatisation and deregulation. opposition to this policy is growing throughout the eu. i note that in the reports, particularly those by mr rodrigues, there are attempts to criticise the policy pursued and some arguments in favour of stimulus measures. however, it is not clear enough for me to vote in favour.
‒ we are in favour of raising minimum wages in the eu to reduce poverty and combat wage dumping. unlike in most countries, there is no statutory minimum wage in sweden, but wages are set by collective agreements between the social partners without direct political interference. we therefore believe that minimum wages should not be regulated by law, but that national practices in each individual member state must be taken into account in order to strengthen collective agreements and workers' rights in the different wage systems.

in writing. - i voted against the eu-tunisia free trade agreement. the resolution deals with the opening of negotiations on a comprehensive trade agreement with tunisia. the country is in dire economic straits after the terrorist attacks in sousse, which basically stopped all tourism. trade with tunisia is therefore positive. however, the proposed agreement will cover, inter alia, the service sector, public procurement and agriculture, and may benefit eu companies rather than the tunisian economy. that is the wrong direction for economic policy. i want to see an economic policy that focuses on debt cancellations for tunisia, because that is a better way to support the country's economy, both in the short and long term - an economy that is based on solidarity and not on free trade.
‒ i welcome the opening of negotiations on a free trade agreement between the eu and tunisia and hope that the agreement will be ambitious, creating great opportunities for growth and jobs, not only for tunisia but also for the eu. i have chosen to vote in favour of this proposal, even though the measures for a temporary duty-free import quota for olive oil from tunisia which parliament has just approved were watered down. i hope that these temporary measures will be part of the future free trade agreement.
the rapporteur. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. i would like to begin by thanking all the shadow rapporteurs for a fantastic job. this is a complicated and large team, and without your broad support and your skill, we would not have achieved the good results we have. i would also like to thank the european commission, which has shown real skill in terms of creativity and flexibility in the negotiations. i would also like to thank the latvian presidency in particular. its dedication to this issue has given us a unique opportunity to reach an agreement which is very pleasing to parliament. the result was the first european law to establish the link between animal welfare, animal health and public health. all in accordance with the principle of 'one health'. the infectious diseases that the law is intended to address, such as avian influenza, also threaten human health. the same is true of the spread of antibiotic-resistant bacteria. all in all, this new law gives us much better preparedness and better tools to deal with future outbreaks and to focus on prevention, prevention. the core requirements of the act also include the obligation for each animal keeper to apply the principles of good animal husbandry and responsible use of veterinary medicinal products. in other words, in the future it will be much more difficult to mask poor animal husbandry with antibiotics. the law also addresses the problem of stray dogs in europe. the national authorities are obliged, when taking measures to control the spread of infection to wild and feral animals, to avoid any unnecessary pain and suffering to the animals. the eu is also proposing to introduce a new system of registration of breeders and sellers of dogs, and to give the european commission a mandate to adopt eu rules on dog registration should the need arise. in addition, the horizontal and therefore extra complicated issue of delegated and implementing acts has been successfully resolved. the result is that parliament retains power over the political issues, the most important of which concerns the list of diseases to be covered by the animal health act, while the technical decisions are made on a scientific basis. this and much more is now part of the package we are voting on tomorrow. this law will be an important step in the fight against antibiotic resistance in animals and humans and in the environment. more than 50 years of eu legislation divided into 39 different pieces of legislation will now be replaced by a clear framework with clear principles and objectives. it is a shining example of the fact that it is possible to simplify the eu's animal rules. my predecessor, marit paulsen, has been doing most of the work on this, and i am grateful to have had the privilege of completing this work. recently, the agreed law was approved unanimously by the committee on agriculture. i hope that you too, ladies and gentlemen, will vote in favour of this important agreement tomorrow.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i wholeheartedly support the new animal health act. it's a step in the right direction for food safety. antibiotic resistance has become a matter of life and death for human health. many consumers are concerned about the residues of medicines in animals when they eat meat. i welcome the fact that the legislation links animal health and human health and that the focus is on preventing infectious diseases in animals rather than curing animals that are sick. it is also important to simplify the rules and focus on measures to reduce the use of antibiotics and treat only those animals that are sick. the uniform and fair use of medicines is also important for our farmers to compete on equal terms. mr president, it seems that it is time for all member states to implement the responsible use of veterinary medicinal products. (the speaker agreed to answer a question (blue card) in accordance with rule 162 (8) of the rules of procedure).
blue-card answer. – yes, as i said, many people are really concerned about the excessive use of antibiotics, both in animals and in human beings. this regulation is about animals, but the link has finally been clearly made, based on science, that it can be transferred. the antibiotics and the medicines that are given to animals can be transferred to human beings. that is why it is so important.
the rapporteur. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. thank you, colleagues, for your support and your comments. let me just emphasize once again the importance of this agreement. this is the first time that a european law has established the link between animal welfare, animal health and public health. the new law will give us better preparedness and better tools to deal with serious disease outbreaks in the future. there is a lot of suffering in both humans and animals that can be avoided and, to add to that, a lot of money can be saved for society in the form of reduced health care costs. money that in these times can be used for other things. the law, if passed tomorrow, will be an important step in the fight against antibiotic resistance in both humans and animals and in our environment. i would also like to mention that it is very gratifying that a whole range of key organisations have supported the law, from the european veterinary association to copa-cogeca, the european animal welfare movement and many others who have supported this law, which shows the breadth of our agreement. i sincerely hope that tomorrow parliament will embrace this law with broad agreement. i would like to thank you for your cooperation, both the shadow rapporteur, the commissioner and the commission, the council and all of you who have contributed to the work on this law.
madam president, i think it is important to underline one thing and that is that the data-driven economy is nothing new. that change has already taken place, and in those parts of our economies that are the most data-driven we see most of the growth and development of prosperity. that is how things are, so let us not talk about this as something new and magic. it is rather about ensuring that we can be the best at processing data, because data is knowledge. knowledge can be distributed, allocated and used in a way that we have never seen before in the modern economy. for us this is much more about catching up regarding cyber—security and securing the free flow of information, combined with the rules on privacy, consumer legislation and copyright; adapting our society to be a strong society benefiting from these opportunities that are already there. i think it is quite easy in one way and that is that, if we want to, we can decide that we should be the best. of course, earlier speakers, mr schaffhauser and others, think we are living in the 19th century. we are not. we can decide to take the lead, have the best 5g, the best fibre, open up the best capacities and enable our society to be the best. that is a decision we can take and i think we should take that decision. (the speaker agreed to take a blue-card question under rule 162(8))
blue-card answer.– i appreciate that question very much because it gives me the opportunity to underline another thing. data is not a magic digital issue. it is knowledge, and knowledge has the character that it can be distributed very rapidly and it can be divided very many times. i can give you knowledge and you can give me knowledge and we will still have the same knowledge with us. that is the magic of knowledge, it is not about data. my feeling and understanding is that most of the data is, for example, the internet of things and the internet of everything – that is not personal data. but in the data-driven economy we can safeguard the personal data that we want to safeguard at the same time as we can increase the levels of cyber security, and still on the aggregated level use the data for the development of health, research, science. a lot of people over there and maybe over there do not want that, but i think we can reach a common understanding on how to use data, the knowledge, safeguarding privacy but also safeguarding european leadership.

in writing. - i've put my vote down. the report is well-intentioned in that it seeks to increase the availability of fruit and milk to school children. it is about creating better nutrition and food knowledge, but there are also strong lobbying interests from the dairy industry that want more eu funds. in addition, a substantial part of the funds will be used to promote and market the eu as a project. the result is a report with good intentions but which actually maximises the benefits to the dairy industry and marketing for the eu. that's not enough to vote for the motion, and that's why i've abstained.
‒ the draft law aims to bring together the eu school milk and school fruit programmes into a single programme. in addition, in the context of the merger of the programmes, additional products have been added to fresh milk. simplifying legislation is commendable, but i do not think it is the eu's responsibility to be responsible for school milk and school fruit. the member states must be responsible here. in view of this, i have decided to vote against the report as a whole.
in writing. we swedish social democrats in the european parliament have long had a negative attitude towards european market measures for agricultural products, which has been clear when we voted on related budget items. we still think that the distribution of agricultural products and milk etc. should not be a european issue and have not changed our basic attitude to this. however, since the regulation is a merger of several programmes already in place, for the sake of reducing administrative burdens and simplification, we have supported improved criteria for the programme.
‒ the draft law, which aims to bring together the eu's school milk and school fruit programmes into a single programme, is designed to help schools offer fresh food to children across the eu. i welcome the purpose of the draft law but do not think that this is a matter for the eu, but that it should be up to the member states to meet it. i therefore think that we should vote against the proposal in principle. however, the situation in many member states, as a result of the economic crisis, is such that milk and fruit from this programme is the only way for many children to get these products into school. that is why i have decided to abstain in this vote.

the european parliament's committee on women's rights has called for the eu to be more active in the fight against violence against women. we think it's important to recognize that women and girls fleeing conflict are particularly vulnerable. they often travel alone with their children and are therefore particularly vulnerable to violence, even when they reach europe. the report draws attention to the need for separate sleeping areas, toilets and shower rooms. it also called for the provision of care, including sexual and reproductive health care, and safe spaces where women can leave their children during asylum interviews. it also called for training of officials and police in gender-based violence to enable them to identify and prevent victims of trafficking and sexual exploitation at an early stage. the member states are invited to implement the common rules on asylum and the rules on combating trafficking. the report also calls for the eu to take action to ensure that the rights of asylum seekers are respected and that the eu is able to provide for the return of refugees.
on behalf of the alde group.– madam president, during the last few months this continent has been faced with an unprecedented number of refugees fleeing from conflicts and oppression. the response from national leaders has frankly been quite pathetic. we have seen calls for systematic mass refoulement of refugees at our borders, in obvious contravention of international humanitarian laws and the treaties and laws of the union. we have seen the reintroduction of internal border controls despite calculations from the bertelsmann institute indicating that only the financial cost of the collapse of schengen could be as high as eur 1.4 trillion over a 10-year period. we have seen member states engaging in a race to the bottom with regard to the reception standards for refugees, hoping that this would push them elsewhere. but what we have not witnessed, however, is european leadership and a willingness to move towards the only solution that can in the long-term return sovereignty and control of the situation to the peoples of europe. in other words, joint action with the community method to ensure a fair, reasonable division of the responsibilities for the people fleeing. what we need is for us to manage our borders together, to share the responsibility for receiving refugees together, to find ways to repatriate those that do not meet the criteria for international protection, and that we do this together under dignified conditions. commissioner avramopoulos and first vice-president timmermans, keep up the good work within the commission and propose now a true european solution, not least in the upcoming dublin reforms. it is high time for the commission and this parliament to take the lead, together with a few courageous leaders we still can count on in council. why? because europe simply deserves it today.
